- **prototype**: Use when a design question is best answered by building something small and throwing it away: whether a state model or a piece of logic holds up against awkward cases, or what a screen should look like. Fixes the question first, picks the shape from it, keeps the prototype visibly disposable, and decides at the end what is lifted into the real code, what is kept as a record and what is never merged. ([techmefr/mentis](https://github.com/techmefr/mentis/tree/HEAD/skills/prototype))

Use whenever the user wants the *why* behind code that's already on disk, the prompt history of a file, or the con… ([mattfogel/prov](https://github.com/mattfogel/prov/tree/HEAD/skills/prov) · ★1)
- **prov-lineage**: Capture W3C PROV-O data lineage for DERIVED RDF, or explain a missing BGP target binding: record CONSTRUCT/DESCRIBE, SPARQL UPDATE, and reasoner-materialization lineage; under the opt-in `why-not` feature, report exactly which grounded BGP triple patterns are absent. Off by default; does not touch sparq-core/sparq-engine's lean build. ([sparq-org/sparq](https://github.com/sparq-org/sparq/tree/HEAD/skills/prov-lineage) · ★18)
- **prov-o-provenance**: Use when modeling, recording or querying provenance with W3C PROV and PROV-O — entities, activities, agents, wasDerivedFrom, wasAttributedTo, qualified relations, revisions, bundles, provenance of provenance. ([Getty/skills](https://github.com/Getty/skills/tree/HEAD/semantic-web/prov-o-provenance) · ★4)

Use only when explicitly invoked as $prove-before-pilot or routed by Better Work Loop. Do not approve a pilot or production use. ([luka-duj/skills-for-better-work](https://github.com/luka-duj/skills-for-better-work/tree/HEAD/skills/prove-before-pilot) · ★4)
- **prove-before-pr**: Verify a local Git branch before the user opens a pull request and produce reproducible Pass, Warn, or Fail evidence. Use only when the user explicitly asks to prepare or verify PR readiness, run a preflight, prove a branch is ready, or invokes $prove-before-pr. Do not use for ordinary code review, fixing CI, inspecting an existing remote pull request, or general test requests. ([grgy078033/prove-before-pr](https://github.com/grgy078033/prove-before-pr/tree/HEAD/skills/prove-before-pr) · ★1)
- **prove-before-showing**: Prove a UI or generation feature actually works before asking a human to look at it. Covers A/B-ing an imported design against its source render, driving controls headlessly so a dead button cannot ship, proving the generator really spawns, and launching the real host (VCV Rack, a DAW). Use before any "take a look" on imported UI, plugin shells, or prompt-to-artifact pipelines. ([Generous-Corp/pulp](https://github.com/Generous-Corp/pulp/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/prove-before-showing) · ★23)
- **prove-biomes**: Prove that a change to threejs-biomes really works, by working out from the diff which library exports and demo pages it reaches, running the package tests, comparing the public export list with its snapshot, and driving the demo page in a real browser to a non-blank render with no console errors. Use on your own, without being asked, after any change to packages/biomes, its tests, its README or its demos, before saying it is done. Also use when asked to verify threejs-biomes, prove its demo, or reproduce a bug there. ([AskAlice/threejs-worldgen](https://github.com/AskAlice/threejs-worldgen/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/prove-biomes) · ★4)
- **prove-blast-radius**: Find what a change could break outside its diff, and prove the fact that makes it safe by running real code. Reports confirmed, open and cleared risks. Use when asked what a change could break, for a small diff you don't trust, or as Reviewer E of review-pr. ([h2xd/skills](https://github.com/h2xd/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/prove-blast-radius) · ★1)
- **prove-by-running**: Execution proof that a script or tool works. Use when about to trust a new or changed script, hook, migration or release tool, and always for anything that rewrites git history, publishes or pushes, deletes, or transforms data; also when the user asks "does this actually work", "test this script", "prove it" or "try it on a copy first". Not for reviewing code by reading it, for writing unit tests of a library, or for fixing the faults found. ([justnnc/skills](https://github.com/justnnc/skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/justnnc-skills/skills/prove-by-running))
- **prove-candidate**: Turns a suspected defect into a confirmed, refuted or declaredly plausible finding — a reproduction with a control and one verdict per claim. Use when triaging code-review output, when investigating a reported bug, or before stating in a PR or an issue that something is broken. ([gems-uff/noworkflow](https://github.com/gems-uff/noworkflow/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/prove-candidate) · ★130)
- **prove-change**: Check a branch against its spec and attack its diff from contexts that did not write it, keeping only findings a failing test or driver proves. Use at a branch close, when the user asks whether a branch does what its spec says or wants its open `possible bug:` questions settled, or another skill needs the spec checked by someone other than the implementer. ([Dan86de/skills](https://github.com/Dan86de/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/in-progress/prove-change) · ★1)
- **prove-change**: Proves a change works by running the software as it is meant to run (CLI, pages, API, or a deployed dev environment) for each acceptance criterion, capturing the output or a screenshot, and looking at it: Proven, Failed or Unproven. Use after building, before a merge request goes ready, or when asked to prove, show or demo that something works. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/prove-change) · ★1)
